Conclusion: 作者明确支持：当前与未来 PTA 对各向异性的约束能力可通过完整协方差矩阵与 fastPTA 进行预测，近期有望对若干低阶多极矩达到百分级精度；各向异性与 Hellings-Downs 角相关近似不相关并可独立重建，这有助于确定引力波背景的起源并区分天体物理源与宇宙学源。对脉冲星、磁星、单脉冲、模式转换、计时、自转减慢、偏振或 FRB 的具体联系，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: Pulsar timing array (PTA) observations have recently gathered substantial evidence for the existence of a gravitational wave background in the nHz frequency band. Searching for anisotropies in this signal is key to determining its origin and in particular to distinguish possible astrophysical from cosmological sources. In this work, we assess the sensitivity of current and future pulsar timing arrays to such anisotropies using the full covariance matrix of pulsar timing delays. While current day pulsar timing arrays can only set mildly informative constraints on the dipole and quadrupole, we show that percent-level accuracy for several low multipoles can be achieved in the near future. Moreover, we demonstrate that anisotropies in the gravitational wave background and the Hellings-Downs angular correlation, indicating the presence of gravitational waves, are approximately uncorrelated and can hence be reconstructed independently. These results can be reproduced with fastPTA, a publicly available Python code to forecast the constraining power of PTA configurations.

### [47] [Toward an exact approach to pulsar timing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10299)
*Carleo, Amodio,Perrodin, Delphine,Possenti, Andrea*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 该文针对伴星为恒星质量或中等质量黑洞的双脉冲星，用数值算法计算传播延迟的全广义相对论值，发现其仅依赖相对轨道半长轴与黑洞质量；在后牛顿近似与全GR之间的最大差异随伴星质量增大而显著增大，且轨道运动的相对论修正会强烈影响传播延迟。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 脉冲星计时通过比较观测到达时间与理论模型预测到达时间来推断物理信息并约束对广义相对论的修正，而传播延迟通常按后牛顿近似计算；近期证据表明，当伴星为超大质量黑洞时该近似可能不再足够，偏差可达几秒，因此需要分析伴星为恒星质量或中等质量黑洞的双脉冲星情形。

Method: 摘要指出，作者针对伴星为恒星质量或中等质量黑洞的双脉冲星，使用数值算法计算传播延迟的全广义相对论值，并与基于后牛顿近似的方法进行比较；还考虑了轨道运动的相对论修正。具体观测数据、望远镜或仪器、频段、时间跨度、样本量及统计分析方法，摘要未说明。

Result: 摘要报告，全GR值仅依赖相对轨道的半长轴和黑洞伴星质量。当伴星质量足够大（$100M_{\odot}$）时，即使对于大双星系统（$\sim10^{16}\ \mathrm{cm}$），两种方法的最大差异仍显著（$\sim10^{-7}\ \mathrm{s}$）；当质量达到$10^{5}M_{\odot}$时，差异增至$\sim10^{-4}\ \mathrm{s}$。此外，轨道运动的相对论修正能够强烈影响传播延迟的数值。

Conclusion: 作者得出结论：在未来，这些系统中应以更精确的方法取代后牛顿公式，尤其是在SKA等新的大型望远镜带来未来发现的前景下。摘要指出此类双脉冲星的发现与计时是SKA的关键目标；对于更广泛的磁星、快速射电暴或单脉冲发射等联系，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: The pulsar timing technique, which compares the observed arrival times of electromagnetic radiation from a pulsar with the predicted arrival times derived from a theoretical model of the pulsar system, is used in pulsar astronomy to infer a multitude of physical information and to constrain possible corrections to general relativity (GR). The propagation delay is usually computed using formulas based on a post-Newtonian approach, for both the light trajectory and the orbital motion. However, evidence has recently emerged that this approximation may no longer be sufficient when the companion object is a supermassive black hole; deviations from a full GR computation of the propagation delay can reach a few seconds. In this paper, we analyze the case of binary pulsars with a stellar or intermediate black hole companion, whose discovery and timing are key goals of SKA. With a numerical algorithm, we have found that in this case, the full GR value depends only on the semimajor axis of the relative orbit and on the mass of the black hole companion. If the mass of the latter is sufficiently large (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mn>100</mml:mn><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>), the maximum difference between the two approaches is significant (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mo>~</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>7</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>s</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>) even for large binaries (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mo>~</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>16</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>cm</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>), and increases up to <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mo>~</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>4</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>s</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula> when the mass is <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>5</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>. We also consider relativistic corrections to the orbital motion, and discover that they can strongly affect the value of the propagation delay. We conclude that in the future, post-Newtonian formulas should be replaced with a more accurate approach in these systems, especially in view of future discoveries made by new large telescopes such as SKA.

</details>

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 该工作用贝叶斯方法、结合 PSR J0030+0451、PSR J0740+6620、PSR J0437-4715 的质量-半径测量以及 GW170817、GW190814 的约束来限制 strangeon matter 的固态，结果表明当前质量-半径测量偏好更大的夸克数 $N_q$，并支持 strangeon 形成稳定束缚态且 $N_q=18$、在色、味和自旋空间中对称，具有相对较强的贝叶斯证据。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 致密星特征密度下致密物质的 EOS 高度不确定。LIGO/Virgo 的致密星旋近观测与 NICER 的质量和半径测量为研究该 EOS 提供了有价值的工具；本文据此在 strangeon matter 作为体强物质基态假定基本单元的框架下，约束其固态并考察假定的有限夸克数 $N_q$。

Method: 采用贝叶斯统计方法约束 strangeon matter 的固态，纳入 PSR J0030+0451、PSR J0740+6620 的质量和半径测量以及近期 $1.4M_\odot$ 脉冲星 PSR J0437-4715 的数据，并加入引力波事件 GW170817 和 GW190814 的约束；在 strangeon 中夸克数有限（$N_q$）的先验假设下进行分析，并比较了三参数模型与两参数模型的后验 EOS 参数空间。摘要未说明具体观测频段、时间跨度、样本量和统计分析方法细节。

Result: 当前质量-半径测量偏好更大的 $N_q$，结果支持 strangeon 形成稳定束缚态且 $N_q=18$、在色、味和自旋空间中对称的情形，并具有相对较强的贝叶斯证据。三参数与两参数模型在相同观测约束下给出一致的预测。对 PSR J0030+0451、PSR J0740+6620、PSR J0437-4715、GW170817 和 GW190814 的联合分析给出的最大质量最概然值在 90% 置信水平下为 $3.58_{-0.12}^{+0.16}M_\odot$（三参数）和 $3.65_{-0.16}^{+0.18}M_\odot$（两参数）；$1.4M_\odot$ 与 $2.1M_\odot$ 恒星半径分别为 $12.04_{-0.31}^{+0.27}\ \mathrm{km}$（$12.16_{-0.31}^{+0.26}\ \mathrm{km}$）和 $13.43_{-0.32}^{+0.31}\ \mathrm{km}$（$13.60_{-0.34}^{+0.29}\ \mathrm{km}$）；$1.4M_\odot$ 恒星的潮汐形变率 $\Lambda_{1.4}$ 为 $205_{-32.53}^{+32.49}$（$212.36_{-32.66}^{+53.71}$）。

Conclusion: 作者认为这些结果可能影响多夸克态的研究，并有可能增进对非微扰强相互作用的理解。关于该结论与脉冲星、磁星、单脉冲发射或 FRB 现象的直接联系，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: The observations of compact star inspirals from LIGO/Virgo, combined with mass and radius measurements from NICER, provide a valuable tool to study the highly uncertain equation of state (EOS) of dense matter at the densities characteristic of compact stars. In this work, we use a Bayesian statistical method to constrain the solid states of strange-cluster matter, called strangeon matter, as the putative basic units of the ground state of bulk strong matter, incorporating the mass and radius measurements of PSR <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>J</mml:mi><mml:mn>0030</mml:mn><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>0451</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, PSR <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>J</mml:mi><mml:mn>0740</mml:mn><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>6620</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, and the recent data for the <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mn>1.4</mml:mn><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> pulsar PSR J0437-4715. We also include constraints from gravitational wave events GW170817 and GW190814. Under the prior assumption of a finite number of quarks in a strangeon, <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>N</mml:mi><mml:mi>q</mml:mi></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>, our analysis reveals that current mass-radius measurements favor a larger <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>N</mml:mi><mml:mi>q</mml:mi></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>. Specifically, the results support the scenario where a strangeon forms a stable bound state with <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>N</mml:mi><mml:mi>q</mml:mi></mml:msub><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:mn>18</mml:mn></mml:math></inline-formula>, symmetric in color, flavor, and spin spaces, with a relatively strong Bayesian evidence. The comparative analyses of the posterior EOS parameter spaces derived from the three-parameter model and two-parameter model demonstrate a consistent prediction under identical observational constraints. In particular, our results reveal that the most probable values of the maximum mass are found to be <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msubsup><mml:mn>3.58</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.12</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.16</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msubsup><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msubsup><mml:mn>3.65</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.16</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.18</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msubsup><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>) at the 90% confidence level for three-parameter (two-parameter) EOS, based on the joint analysis of PSR <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>J</mml:mi><mml:mn>0030</mml:mn><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>0451</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, PSR <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>J</mml:mi><mml:mn>0740</mml:mn><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>6620</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, PSR J0437-4715, GW170817, and GW190814. Correspondingly, the radii for <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mn>1.4</mml:mn><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> and <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mn>2.1</mml:mn><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> stars are <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msubsup><mml:mn>12.04</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.31</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.27</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msubsup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>km</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula> (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msubsup><mml:mn>12.16</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.31</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.26</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msubsup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>km</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>), and <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msubsup><mml:mn>13.43</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.32</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.31</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msubsup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>km</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula> (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msubsup><mml:mn>13.60</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.34</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.29</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msubsup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>km</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>), respectively. ### [49] [Highly-accurate neutron star modeling in the Hartle-Thorne approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.19400)
*Conde-Ocazionez, Carlos,Yin, Tuojin,Noronha-Hostler, Jaquelyn,Yunes, Nicolás*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 本文在Hartle-Thorne近似下将缓慢旋转、孤立、无磁化中子星的慢旋转展开扩展至自旋频率七阶，获得外部度规各阶的精确闭式解析解，并据此导出至七阶的质量与质量流标量多极矩，从而可用于更精确计算X射线脉冲轮廓以及I-Love-Q和三毛关系。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 未来如NICER和LOFT等X射线任务以及地面探测器的引力波观测将提供关于中子星的新认识，而解释这些精确观测需要中子星引力场的精确模型。摘要指出，本文是两部分系列论文的第一篇，旨在构建缓慢旋转、孤立且无磁化中子星的微扰方程，以提升慢旋转近似的阶数。

Abstract: Future x-ray missions, such as NICER and LOFT, together with gravitational-wave observations from ground-based detectors, will provide new insights into neutron stars. Interpreting accurate observations in the future will require accurate models of their gravitational fields. In this first paper of a two-part series, we construct the perturbation equations for slowly rotating, isolated, and unmagnetized neutron stars, extending the Hartle-Thorne approximation to seventh order in a slow-rotation expansion. We obtain exact, closed-form, analytical solutions for the exterior metric at each order in spin. From these solutions, we derive expressions for the mass and mass-current scalar multipole moments, <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>ℓ</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> and <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>S</mml:mi><mml:mo>ℓ</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>, respectively, up to seventh order in spin frequency, using two distinct methods. This high-order expansion allows us to calculate second-, fourth-, and sixth-order relative spin corrections to the observed mass and moment of inertia; second- and fourth-order relative spin corrections to the quadrupole and octopole moments; second-order relative spin corrections to the hexadecapole and dotriacontapole moments; and leading-order-in-spin expressions for the hexacontatetrapole and hectoicosaoctapole moments. Going to seventh order in the spin-frequency approximation will enable very precise calculations of x-ray pulse profiles, as well as the I-Love-Q and three-hair relations for slowly rotating neutron stars. These results will be valuable for breaking parameter degeneracies in future multimessenger observations.

Abstract: As we move into an era of more sensitive pulsar timing array datasets, we may be able to resolve individual gravitational wave sources from the stochastic gravitational wave background. This motivates the search for generic gravitational-wave bursts in a signal-agnostic way. However, these searches are computationally prohibitive due to the expansive parameter space. In this paper we present QuickBurst, an algorithm with a redefined likelihood that lets us expedite Markov chain Monte Carlo sampling for a subset of the signal parameters by avoiding repeated calculations of costly inner products. This results in an overall speedup factor of <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mo>~</mml:mo><mml:mn>200</mml:mn></mml:math></inline-formula> on realistic simulated datasets, which is sufficient to make generic gravitational-wave burst searches feasible on current and growing pulsar timing array datasets.

Abstract: High-precision astrometry offers a promising approach to detect low-frequency gravitational waves, complementing pulsar timing array (PTA) observations. Analytical, covariant expressions for this response are derived, accounting for the presence of a possible dipolar anisotropy in the SGWB. We identify the optimal estimator for extracting SGWB information from astrometric observations and examine how sensitivity to SGWB properties varies with the sky positions of stars and pulsars. Using representative examples of current PTA capabilities and near-future astrometric sensitivity, we demonstrate that cross-correlating astrometric and PTA data can improve constraints on SGWB properties, compared to PTA data alone. The improvement is quantified through Fisher forecasts for the SGWB amplitude, spectral tilt, and dipolar anisotropy amplitude. In the future, such joint constraints could play a crucial role in identifying the origin of SGWB signals detected by PTAs.

*van Haasteren, Rutger*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 该工作从第一性原理分析PTA中用于随机引力波背景探测的 $p$ 值计算方法，推导出探测统计量分布及相应 $p$ 值的解析表达式，结论是scrambling方法并非模型无关，并提出应改用基于广义 $\chi^2$ 分布的严格贝叶斯与频率派方法。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: PTA项目已报告多种暗示数据中存在随机引力波背景的证据线索，其中关键一类是对脉冲星间相关性敏感的探测统计量。为评估观测信号在零假设 $H_0$ 下偶然出现的可能性，需要计算 $p$ 值，但PTA无法从 $H_0$ 中经验性抽取样本，只能借助各种近似技术，例如被称为模型无关的scrambling变换。

Method: 从第一性原理出发研究scrambling方法与探测统计量；严格分析文献中所有用于该探测统计量 $p$ 值的计算方法，并推导探测统计量分布及相应 $p$ 值的解析表达式；推导在真实数据上评估探测统计量广义 $\chi^2$ 分布的高效表达式。所讨论的 $p$ 值方法学是通用的，而关于特定探测统计量的讨论适用于用PTA探测随机引力波背景。摘要未说明具体观测数据、频段、时间跨度或样本。

Conclusion: 作者认为，由于PTA无法从 $H_0$ 经验采样，单次数据实现下的结果必然总是模型依赖的，任何分析都需接受这一点；因此不应采用scrambling方法，而应提倡严格的贝叶斯和频率派 $p$ 值计算方法，其评估依赖广义 $\chi^2$ 分布，这一观点与文献中已有的后验预测 $p$ 值方法一致。根据摘要无法判断该方法与单个脉冲星、磁星或FRB观测的具体联系。

Abstract: Pulsar Timing Array (PTA) projects have reported various lines of evidence suggesting the presence of a stochastic gravitational wave (GW) background in their data. One key line of evidence involves a detection statistic sensitive to interpulsar correlations, such as those induced by GWs. A <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>p</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-value is then calculated to assess how unlikely it is for the observed signal to arise under the null hypothesis <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>H</mml:mi><mml:mn>0</mml:mn></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>, purely by chance. However, PTAs cannot empirically draw samples from <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>H</mml:mi><mml:mn>0</mml:mn></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>. As a workaround, various techniques are used in the literature to approximate <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>p</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-values under <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>H</mml:mi><mml:mn>0</mml:mn></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>. One such technique, which has been characterized as a model-independent method, is the use of ``scrambling'' transformations that modify the data to cancel out pulsar correlations, thereby simulating realizations from <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>H</mml:mi><mml:mn>0</mml:mn></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>. In this work, scrambling methods and the detection statistic are investigated from first principles. The <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>p</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-value methodology that is discussed is general, but the discussions regarding a specific detection statistic apply to the detection of a stochastic background of gravitational waves with PTAs. All methods in the literature to calculate <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>p</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-values for such a detection statistic are rigorously analyzed, and analytical expressions are derived for the distribution of the detection statistic and the corresponding <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>p</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-values. All this leads to the conclusion that scrambling methods are not model independent and thus not completely empirical. With a single realization of data our results are necessarily always model dependent, which any analysis will need to accept. Instead of scrambling approaches, rigorous Bayesian and Frequentist <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>p</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-value calculation methods are advocated, the evaluation of which depend on the generalized <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msup><mml:mi>χ</mml:mi><mml:mn>2</mml:mn></mml:msup></mml:math></inline-formula> distribution. This view is consistent with the posterior predictive <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>p</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-value approach that is already in the literature. Efficient expressions are derived to evaluate the generalized <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msup><mml:mi>χ</mml:mi><mml:mn>2</mml:mn></mml:msup></mml:math></inline-formula> distribution of the detection statistic on real data. It is highlighted that no Frequentist <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>p</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-values have been calculated correctly in the PTA literature to date.

</details>


### [54] [Accelerated Bayesian inference for pulsar timing arrays: Normalizing flows for rapid model comparison across stochastic gravitational-wave background sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.04211)
*Lai, Junrong,Li, Changhong*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 该文提出基于归一化流（NF）的机器学习框架，用于脉冲星计时阵列中随机引力波背景（SGWB）源模型的贝叶斯模型比较，在NANOGrav 15年数据集上以约20小时（含训练）完成推断，而MCMC约需10天，并保持严格的模型选择准确性。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 脉冲星计时阵列（PTAs）近期对纳赫兹随机引力波背景（SGWBs）的探测，有望提供关于其天体物理与宇宙学起源的独特见解；但传统MCMC方法对大型数据集过于昂贵，因此需要能够加速贝叶斯推断并支撑SGWB源模型比较的方法。

Method: 采用基于归一化流（NF）的机器学习框架加速PTA分析中的贝叶斯推断，并在PTA数据集（NANOGrav 15年）上训练NF架构，借助学习到的调和平均估计器进行直接证据估计。示例包括10种常规SGWB源模型，如超大质量黑洞双星、幂律谱、宇宙弦、畴壁、标量诱导引力波、一阶相变以及双情景/暴胀引力波；每个模型联合推断20个红噪声参数（10颗脉冲星）和两个SGWB参数。

Conclusion: 作者认为，这种用于SGWB源比较的可扩展技术对于未来PTA扩展将至关重要，下一代阵列如SKA可能在不牺牲物理可解释性的情况下提供显著的效率提升。根据摘要无法判断其对脉冲星、磁星、单脉冲、快速射电暴等具体议题的物理可迁移性。

Abstract: The recent detection of nanohertz stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds (SGWBs) by pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) promises unique insights into astrophysical and cosmological origins. However, traditional Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) approaches become prohibitively expensive for large datasets. We employ a normalizing flow (NF)-based machine learning framework to accelerate Bayesian inference in PTA analyses. For the first time, we perform Bayesian model comparison across SGWB source models in the framework of machine learning by training NF architectures on the PTA dataset (NANOGrav 15 year) and enabling direct evidence estimation via learned harmonic mean estimators. Our examples include 10 conventional SGWB source models such as supermassive black hole binaries, Power-Law spectrum, cosmic strings, domain walls, scalar-induced GWs, first-order phase transitions, and dual scenario/inflationary gravitational wave. Several dissipation mechanisms have been proposed, each operating with different efficiencies depending on the region of the star. Simulations are performed both at constant and variable temperatures. In the latter case, a strategy that decouples the magnetic and thermal evolution is employed, enabling efficient thermal modeling across a range of initial magnetic field strengths. At constant temperature, we obtain the expected result where neutrons reach diffusive equilibrium, the Lorentz force is balanced by chemical potential gradients of charged particles, and the magnetic field satisfies a nonlinear Grad-Shafranov equation. When thermal evolution is included, fields <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>B</mml:mi><mml:mo>≳</mml:mo><mml:mn>5</mml:mn><mml:mo>×</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>15</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>G</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula> can balance ambipolar heating and neutrino cooling, delaying the evolution over <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mo>~</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>3</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mo>[</mml:mo><mml:mi>B</mml:mi><mml:mo>/</mml:mo><mml:mo>(</mml:mo><mml:mn>5</mml:mn><mml:mo>×</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>15</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>G</mml:mi><mml:mo>)</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mo>]</mml:mo><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>6</mml:mn><mml:mo>/</mml:mo><mml:mn>5</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>yr</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>. Although the surface luminosity is enhanced compared with passive cooling, the heating from ambipolar diffusion alone is insufficient to fully explain the persistent x-ray emission observed in magnetars.

Abstract: The detection of a gravitational wave background in the nanohertz frequency range from pulsar timing array (PTA) observations offers new insights into evolution of the early Universe. In this work we analyze gravitational wave data from PPTA, EPTA, and NANOGrav, as arising from a supercooled first-order phase transition within a hidden sector, characterized by a broken <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>U</mml:mi><mml:mo>(</mml:mo><mml:mn>1</mml:mn><mml:msub><mml:mo>)</mml:mo><mml:mi>X</mml:mi></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> gauge symmetry. Several previous works have discussed challenges in producing observable PTA signal from supercooled phases transitions. We discuss these challenges and show how they are overcome by inclusion in part of the proper thermal history of the hidden and the visible sectors. The analysis of this work demonstrates that thermal histories of hidden and visible sectors profoundly influence the gravitational wave power spectrum, an aspect not previously explored in the literature. Further, the analysis of this work suggests that supercooled phase transitions not only align with the pulsar timing array observations but also show promise for gravitational wave detection by future gravitational wave detectors. Our analysis shows that the dominant contribution to the gravitational wave power spectrum for PTA signal comes from bubble collision while the sound wave and turbulence contributions are highly suppressed. It is also found that all of the PTA events are of detonation type while deflagration and hybrid events are absent. The analysis presented in this work provides a robust framework for further investigations on the origin of gravitational wave power spectrum in the early Universe and for their experimental observation in the future.

Abstract: Fast magnetosonic waves are one of the two low-frequency plasma modes that can exist in a neutron star magnetosphere. It was recently realized that these waves may become nonlinear within the magnetosphere and steepen into some of the strongest shocks in the universe. These shocks, when in the appropriate parameter regime, may emit GHz radiation in the form of precursor waves. We present the first global particle-in-cell simulations of the nonlinear steepening of fast magnetosonic waves in a dipolar magnetosphere, and quantitatively demonstrate the strong plasma acceleration in the upstream of these shocks. In these simulations, we observe the production of precursor waves in a finite angular range. Using analytic scaling relations, we predict the expected frequency, power, and duration of this emission. Within a reasonable range of progenitor wave parameters, these precursor waves can reproduce many aspects of FRB observations.

We find that this source population exhibits a luminosity function with a mean <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>γ</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>-ray luminosity of <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mo>⟨</mml:mo><mml:msub><mml:mrow><mml:mi>L</mml:mi></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mi>γ</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:msub><mml:mo>⟩</mml:mo><mml:mo>~</mml:mo><mml:mn>6</mml:mn><mml:mo>×</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mrow><mml:mn>10</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mn>32</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mrow><mml:mi>erg</mml:mi><mml:mo>/</mml:mo><mml:mi>s</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> (integrated above 0.1 GeV). If the GCE were generated by millisecond pulsars with the same luminosity function, we find that <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mo>~</mml:mo><mml:mn>20</mml:mn></mml:math></inline-formula> such sources from the inner Galaxy population should have already been detected by Fermi and included in the 3PC. Given the lack of such observed sources, we exclude the hypothesis that the GCE is generated by pulsars with the same luminosity function as those in the Galactic disk with a significance of <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mn>3.4</mml:mn><mml:mi>σ</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>. We conclude that either less than 39% of the GCE is generated by pulsars, or that the millisecond pulsars in the inner Galaxy are at least 5 times less luminous on average than those found in the Galactic disk.

Abstract: We investigate observational signatures of ultralight vector dark matter with masses <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>m</mml:mi><mml:mo>~</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>24</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mi>--</mml:mi><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>22</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>eV</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> in pulsar timing arrays, taking into account general polarization states of the vector field. Abstract: Searches for continuous gravitational waves from isolated compact objects and those in binary systems aim to detect nonaxisymmetric, deformed neutron stars at particular locations in the Galaxy or all sky. DECIGO will thus open a new window to probe highly deformed compact objects and over half of the known pulsars, both of which are currently inaccessible to ground-based detectors.

Abstract: The detection of a stochastic signal by recent pulsar timing array (PTA) collaborations, including NANOGrav, PPTA, <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>EPTA</mml:mi><mml:mo>+</mml:mo><mml:mi>InPTA</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, CPTA, and MPTA, has opened a new window to explore gravitational waves (GWs) at nanohertz frequencies. Utilizing PTA datasets, we provide constraints on the local-type tensor non-Gaussianity parameter <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>F</mml:mi><mml:mi>NL</mml:mi></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula>. In the presence of a strong magnetic field, Leinson and Perez showed and Baiko and Yakovlev confirmed that the energy levels of electrons and protons are quantized and the direct Urca process allows neutron stars to cool rapidly, even at low density. For the case of magnetic fields <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>B</mml:mi><mml:mo>≳</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mrow><mml:mn>10</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mn>16</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>G</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, we find features in the emissivity due to energy quantization that are not captured by the frequently employed quasiclassical approximation where energy levels are treated as nearly continuous. Resonances can result in amplification of the neutrino emissivity at specific densities compared to a calculation that neglects quantization, particularly at low temperature. These effects are not important for the thermal evolution of an entire neutron star, but may be relevant for phenomena that depend on behavior at specific densities. We present a fully relativistic calculation of the direct Urca rate in a strong magnetic field using the standard V-A weak Lagrangian incorporating mean field nuclear effects and discuss approaches to the numerical challenge the modified wave functions present and a new semianalytic approximation. These tools are also applicable to calculating neutrino opacities in strong magnetic fields in the ejecta of binary neutron star mergers. We calculate the opacities for neutrinos capturing on free nucleons at subsaturation densities and temperatures exceeding an MeV. We find an enhancement to capture processes of the lowest energy neutrinos by an order of magnitude or more due to suppression of electron Pauli blocking in the case of capture on neutrons, and from the effect of the nucleon magnetic moments in the case of capture on protons.

Abstract: Magnetars are neutron stars with superstrong magnetic fields. Some of them (soft-gamma repeaters, SGRs) demonstrate gigantic flares whose nature is still unclear. At the decay phase of such flares, one often observes quasiperiodic oscillations (QPOs), which are treated as stellar oscillations triggered by the flares. We study, for the first time, magnetoelastic oscillations of magnetars possessing toroidal magnetic fields confined in the stellar crust, without imposing axial symmetry of perturbations. We show that the Zeeman effect makes the oscillation spectrum much richer than for axially symmetric oscillations. The main properties of theoretical QPO spectra are discussed, as well as their potential to interpret observations and explore magnetar physics.

Notably, this estimation is significantly lower compared to the currently inferred values for the radius of neutron stars (as they are derived from both theoretical calculations and corresponding observations). In this paper, we focus on the construction of hybrid equations of state (EOSs) for the possible reconciliation of the exotic XTE J1814-338 properties. Our analysis indicates that an equation of state involving a sufficiently strong phase transition could potentially lead to the explanation of the size and mass of such a compact object. By examining the sign of the <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>d</mml:mi><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>/</mml:mo><mml:mi>d</mml:mi><mml:msub><mml:mi>P</mml:mi><mml:mi>c</mml:mi></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> slope, we found that stellar configurations compatible to XTE J1814-338 are only stable for a ``stiff'' low density phase. For a ``soft'' hadronic EOS selection the stability of the resulting configurations may not be satisfied in terms of the turning point criterion. However, a complementary analysis, for the radial oscillations of the aforementioned configurations, indicated their stability when a slow phase conversion is considered.

Abstract: Recent pulsar timing array (PTA) experiments have reported strong evidence of the stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB). In this paper, we construct a concrete Ekpyrosis-bouncing model, and show its capacity to intepret the PTA result without pathologies. Both tensor and scalar perturbations are analyzed with constraints from the current observations.

Abstract: We present a novel method that enables us to estimate the acceleration of individual millisecond pulsars (MSPs) using only their spin period and its time derivative. This updated value for <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>ρ</mml:mi><mml:mrow><mml:mn>0</mml:mn><mml:mo>,</mml:mo><mml:mi>DM</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> is in good agreement with literature values derived from kinematic estimates. <details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 脉冲星计时阵列（PTA）提供了搜寻超轻暗物质（ULDM）的独立方法，其波动性会在射电脉冲到达时间中引起周期性振荡；除引力效应外，ULDM与普通物质的直接耦合还会导致脉冲星自旋涨落和参考钟偏移，从而在PTA中产生可观测效应。已有EPTA第二次数据发布仅基于引力效应表明ULDM不能构成该质量范围内的全部暗物质，本文旨在进一步纳入耦合效应导出耦合系数限制。

Method: 摘要未说明。摘要仅指出本文在推导耦合系数限制时同时考虑引力效应和耦合效应，并使用了European Pulsar Timing Array（EPTA）第二次数据发布；未说明具体观测、仪器、频段、时间跨度、样本、计时或统计分析方法。

Result: EPTA第二次数据发布表明，仅基于引力效应，ULDM不能在该质量范围 m_ϕ ∈ [10^{-24.0}–10^{-23.3}] eV 内构成全部暗物质。本文同时考虑引力与耦合效应后导出了耦合系数限制，结果表明对于较轻质量的ULDM，即约 m_ϕ ≤ 10^{-22} eV，EPTA对这些耦合给出的限制强于此前PTA实验，并建立与其他精密实验（如原子钟测量）相当甚至更紧的限制。摘要未给出具体耦合系数数值、置信水平或统计显著性。

Conclusion: 作者认为，在纳入ULDM与普通物质的直接耦合效应后，EPTA第二次数据发布能够在约 m_ϕ ≤ 10^{-22} eV 的较轻质量区对耦合系数给出比此前PTA实验更强的限制，且与原子钟测量等其他精密实验相当或更紧；这体现了PTA作为搜寻ULDM的独立方法的竞争力。关于该结果对脉冲星自旋涨落、参考钟偏移或其他射电观测量的具体物理含义，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: Pulsar Timing Arrays (PTAs) offer an independent method for searching for ultralight dark matter (ULDM), whose wavelike nature induces periodic oscillations in the arrival times of radio pulses. In addition to this gravitational effect, the direct coupling between ULDM and ordinary matter results in pulsar spin fluctuations and reference clock shifts, leading to observable effects in PTAs. The second data release from the European PTA (EPTA) indicates that ULDM cannot account for all dark matter in the mass range <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msub><mml:mrow><mml:mi>m</mml:mi></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mi>ϕ</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:msub><mml:mo>∈</mml:mo><mml:mo>[</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mrow><mml:mn>10</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>24.0</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mrow><mml:mn>10</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>23.3</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mo>]</mml:mo><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>eV</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> based solely on gravitational effects. In this work, we derive constraints on the coupling coefficients by considering both gravitational and coupling effects. Our results demonstrate that for ULDM with lighter masses, i.e., approximately <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msub><mml:mi>m</mml:mi><mml:mi>ϕ</mml:mi></mml:msub><mml:mo>&lt;=</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>22</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>eV</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula>, EPTA provides stronger constraints on these couplings than previous PTA experiments and establishes comparable or even tighter constraints than other precise experiments, such as the atomic clock measurements.

</details>

Method: 摘要说明，本工作考察此类天体环绕典型恒星（例如太阳）运行时所发射的引力波信号，并将其与一颗可能 PBH 环绕密度更高但更遥远的中子星所产生的引力波进行比较，还估计大量此类 PBH—恒星系统对随机引力波背景的贡献。摘要未说明具体的观测数据、时间跨度、样本、分析流程或统计方法。

Abstract: Primordial black holes (PBHs) with masses between <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>14</mml:mn></mml:msup></mml:math></inline-formula> and <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msup><mml:mrow><mml:mn>10</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mn>20</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>kg</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> are candidates to contribute a substantial fraction of the total dark matter abundance. When in orbit around the center of a star, which can possibly be a completely interior orbit, such objects would emit gravitational waves, as predicted by general relativity. In this work, we examine the gravitational wave signals emitted by such objects when they orbit typical stars, such as the Sun. We show that the magnitude of the waves that could eventually be detected on Earth from a possible PBH orbiting the Sun or a neighboring Sun-like star within our Galaxy can be significantly stronger than those originating from a PBH orbiting a denser but more distant neutron star. Such signals may be detectable by the LISA gravitational wave detector. In addition, we estimate the contribution that a large collection of such PBH-star systems would make to the stochastic gravitational wave background within a range of frequencies to which pulsar timing arrays are sensitive.

</details>


### [79] [Phase transitions in the inner crust of neutron stars within the superfluid band theory: Competition between <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mmultiscripts><mml:mi>S</mml:mi><mml:mn>0</mml:mn><mml:mn>1</mml:mn></mml:mmultiscripts></mml:math></inline-formula> pairing and spin polarization under finite temperature and magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.06250)
*Yoshimura, Kenta,Sekizawa, Kazuyuki*

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 该工作将完全自洽超流核能带理论推广到有限温度和有限磁场情形，并用于中子星物质板状相，预言了中子超流消失、核板“熔化”以及质子和中子自旋极化的临界温度与磁场；还发现中子自旋可在板内和板外发生局域极化而总体仍保持未极化。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 中子星内部必然发生从正常相到超流/超导相以及壳层形成等相变，但要在同一框架下刻画超流/超导、有限温度与磁场效应，需要发展完全微观方法。已有超流核能带理论形式体系局限于特定条件，尚未推广到有限温度和有限磁场系统，因而无法系统探索温度与磁场驱动的核物质相变。

Method: 采用基于超流系统 Kohn-Sham 密度泛函理论、配对采用超流局域密度近似并施加 Bloch 边界条件的超流能带理论。假设沿 $z$ 方向周期变化、沿 $xy$ 方向均匀分布，以描述板状相和均匀核物质；以类似有限温度 Hartree-Fock-Bogoliubov 计算的方式引入有限温度；磁场效应同时考虑相对论性电子朗道能级形成和磁场与核子磁矩耦合。对 $n_B=0.04, 0.05, 0.06, 0.07\ \mathrm{fm}^{-3}$ 的板状相在各种温度与磁场组合下计算。

Result: 无磁场（$B=0$）时，中子超流性在 $k_BT=0.6--0.9\ \mathrm{MeV}$ 附近消失，核板“熔化”即转变为均匀物质发生在 $k_BT=2.5--4.5\ \mathrm{MeV}$ 附近。开启磁场后，质子自旋在 $B=10^{16}\ \mathrm{G}$ 附近极化，而中子自旋在平均意义上直至约 $B=10^{17}\ \mathrm{G}$ 仍未极化；但微观计算显示中子自旋在 $B\approx10^{16}\ \mathrm{G}$ 时已在板内外局域极化，而系统总体保持未极化。该局域极化由中子间 $^1S_0$ 配对与中子-质子自旋相关相互作用的共同作用导致。

Conclusion: 作者表明完全自洽超流核能带理论可有效且实用地描述任意温度和磁场下的中子星物质，并在与现实中子星环境相关的条件下给出了（1）超流到正常转变、（2）壳层形成和（3）自旋极化的临界温度和磁场。摘要未涉及脉冲星、磁星、快速射电暴（FRB）、单脉冲、计时、偏振观测或射电频谱的直接观测联系；根据摘要无法判断其与这些观测主题的具体关联。

Abstract: Background: Phase transitions of matter under changes of external environment such as temperature and magnetic field have attracted great interests to various quantum many-body systems. Several phase transitions must have occurred in neutron stars as well such as transitions from normal to superfluid and superconducting phases and crust formation. While the temperature of a protoneutron star is as high as 10 MeV (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mo>≈</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>11</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>K</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>) or higher, which are above critical temperatures for the emergence of superfluidity and crust formation, it cools rapidly down to 0.1 keV (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mo>≈</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>6</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>K</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>) already after hundreds of years. While ordinary neutron stars have surface magnetic field strength of around <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>12</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>G</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, those having higher magnetic field strength of <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mn>14</mml:mn><mml:mo>--</mml:mo><mml:mn>15</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>G</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> or higher, so-called magnetars, have been observed. To uncover detailed evolution of neutron stars from their birth to later years, it is desired to develop fully microscopic approaches that take into account effects of superfluidity and superconductivity, finite temperature and magnetic field, on the same footing. Purpose: The main purpose of this work is twofold: (1) to extend the formalism of the fully self-consistent superfluid nuclear band theory, developed in our previous work [K. Yoshimura and K. Sekizawa, Phys. Rev. C 109, 065804 (2024)10.1103/PhysRevC.109.065804], for finite-temperature and finite-magnetic-field systems, and (2) to explore possible phase transitions of nuclear matter by varying temperature and magnetic field. Methods: We employ the superfluid band theory which is based on the Kohn-Sham density functional theory for superfluid systems with a local treatment of paring, known as superfluid local density approximation, subjected to the Bloch boundary conditions. We assume periodic spatial variation along <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>z</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula> direction with uniform distribution along <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>x</mml:mi><mml:mi>y</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> direction, allowing us to describe the slab phase as well as uniform nuclear matter. The finite-temperature extension is achieved in a similar manner as a finite-temperature Hartree-Fock-Bogoliubov calculation. Magnetic field effects are introduced taking into account both the Landau levels formation of relativistic electrons and the couplings of the magnetic field with nucleons' magnetic moments. Results: We have performed superfluid band theory calculations for the slab phase of neutron star matter at <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msub><mml:mi>n</mml:mi><mml:mi>B</mml:mi></mml:msub><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.04</mml:mn><mml:mo>,</mml:mo><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mn>0.05</mml:mn><mml:mo>,</mml:mo><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mn>0.06</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, and <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mn>0.07</mml:mn><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:msup><mml:mrow><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>fm</mml:mi></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>3</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> under various sets of temperature and magnetic field. From the results without magnetic field (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>B</mml:mi><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:mn>0</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>), we find that the superfluidity of neutrons disappears at around <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msub><mml:mi>k</mml:mi><mml:mi>B</mml:mi></mml:msub><mml:mi>T</mml:mi><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.6</mml:mn><mml:mo>--</mml:mo><mml:mn>0.9</mml:mn><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>MeV</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, and ``melting'' of nuclear slabs, that is, a structural change into the uniform matter, takes place at around <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msub><mml:mi>k</mml:mi><mml:mi>B</mml:mi></mml:msub><mml:mi>T</mml:mi><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:mn>2.5</mml:mn><mml:mo>--</mml:mo><mml:mn>4.5</mml:mn><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>MeV</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>. By turning on the magnetic field, we find that protons' spin gets polarized at around <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>B</mml:mi><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>16</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>G</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, whereas neutrons' spin is kept unpolarized on average up to around <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>B</mml:mi><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>17</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>G</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>. Intriguingly, our microscopic calculations reveal that neutrons' spin is actually polarized locally inside and outside of the slab already at <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mi>B</mml:mi><mml:mo>≈</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>16</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:mi>G</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>, while keeping the system unpolarized in total. We show that the local polarization of neutrons' spin is caused by an interplay of <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msup><mml:mrow></mml:mrow><mml:mn>1</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:msub><mml:mi>S</mml:mi><mml:mn>0</mml:mn></mml:msub></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> pairing among neutrons and spin-dependent interactions between neutron and protons. Conclusions: We have demonstrated validity and usefulness of the fully self-consistent superfluid nuclear band theory for describing neutron star matter under arbitrary temperature and magnetic field. Critical temperatures and magnetic fields have been predicted for (1) superfluid to normal transition, (2) crust formation, and (3) spin polarization, under conditions relevant to realistic neutron star environments.

Conclusion: 作者认为，限制 PBH 模型方面的未来进展预计将源于 PBH 计算的理论进展，这应有助于解决不同计算方法之间的张力。抽象地看，该工作的方法学意义在于对未来脉冲星计时阵列数据集进行预测性评估，但这是否可迁移至脉冲星、磁星、单脉冲、模式转换或 FRB 等具体主题，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: Pulsar timing arrays are playing a crucial role in the ongoing gravitational wave astronomy revolution. The current evidence for a stochastic gravitational wave background at nHz frequencies offers an opportunity to discover cosmological signals and threatens the observability of other subdominant gravitational waves (GWs). We explore prospects to constrain second-order scalar-induced GWs (SIGWs) associated with enhanced curvature perturbations in the primordial universe, forecasting realistic future pulsar timing array datasets. We assess how the currently observed signal could eventually limit future capabilities to search for GW relics of primordial phenomena and associated phenomenological consequences such as primordial black hole (PBH) formation. Given the sensitivity of PBH abundance to spectral parameters, measuring it remains a challenge for realistic signals. However, future observation could still rule out nearly subsolar mass PBHs formed through standard formation scenarios in some cases. Future progress in constraining PBH models is expected to stem from theoretical advancements in PBH computations, which should help resolve the tension between different computational methods. The analysis is based on and extends the Python code fastPTA.

### [82] [Bayesian inferences on covariant density functionals from multimessenger astrophysical data: Nucleonic models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.20000)

Main category: scix

TL;DR: 在基于密度依赖耦合 CDF 的贝叶斯推断中纳入“黑寡妇”脉冲星 PSR J0952-0607 与超致密低质量天体 HESS J1731-347 数据后，模型可支持高达 2.4--2.5 M⊙ 的致密星最大质量，但对 HESS J1731-347 所需的低密度 EoS 软化仍难以实现。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 已有融合多信使天体物理约束的贝叶斯推断框架被用于约束具有密度依赖介子-核子耦合的 CDF 模型参数，但该框架此前尚未纳入可能具有极端高质量或超小半径的天体，因此其在极端参数下的灵活性与预测能力仍属未知。

Method: 采用基于具有密度依赖耦合的 CDF 的贝叶斯推断框架，天体物理数据除 NICER 与引力波事件的最新多信使约束外，还纳入“黑寡妇”脉冲星 PSR J0952-0607 迄今测得的最高质量和超致密低质量天体 HESS J1731-347 的质量-半径估计；通过分析关键 CS 性质、饱和密度核特性及其相关性的后验分布，考察两个源各自及联合影响的显著性。摘要未说明观测频率、时间跨度与具体采样量。

Result: 在典型天体物理约束之外纳入 PSR J0952--0607 和/或 HESS J1731--347 数据后，系统性贝叶斯分析表明 CDF 模型能够支持更高的 CS 最大质量，最高可达 2.4--2.5 M⊙；但在低密度区域使 EoS 足够软化以容纳 HESS J1731--347 数据仍然具有挑战性。尽管如此，作者能够对 CDF 模型参数空间施加更紧约束，使之与当前核实验和天体物理数据保持一致。

Conclusion: 作者认为，具有密度依赖介子-核子耦合的 CDF 模型涵盖广泛的核与天体物理现象，为解释致密天体提供了稳健的理论框架；但预测的低质量恒星半径下限约为 12 km，源于同位旋矢量自由度受限，可通过扩展 CDF 模型以引入更复杂的对称能密度依赖来改善，或允许低密度退禁闭相变至夸克物质以获得更小半径。该结论属于核物理与致密星 EoS 建模层面；对脉冲星、磁星、单脉冲发射或 FRB 的适用性，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: Background: Bayesian inference frameworks incorporating multimessenger astrophysical constraints have recently been applied to covariant density functional (CDF) models to constrain their parameters. Among these, frameworks utilizing CDFs with density-dependent meson-nucleon couplings furnishing the equation of state (EoS) of compact star (CS) matter have been explored. This class of CDF models has been extensively tested against various nuclear phenomena, providing a highly predictive description of CSs in terms of nuclear characteristics defined in the vicinity of nuclear saturation density and isopin symmetric limit. Purpose: The aforementioned inference framework has not yet incorporated astrophysical objects with potentially extreme high masses or ultrasmall radii among its constraints, leaving its flexibility and predictive power under such extreme parameters still unknown. We aim at exploring the capabilities of this framework to account for these additional data. Method: We apply the Bayesian inference framework based on CDFs with density dependent couplings. The astrophysical data are expanded to include not only the latest multimessenger constraints from the NICER (Neutron star Interior Composition Explorer) telescope and gravitational wave events but also the highest measured mass to date for the ``black widow'' pulsar PSR J0952-0607 and the mass-radius estimates for the ultracompact, low-mass object HESS J1731-347. The significance of the individual impact of these two sources, as well as the broader picture that emerges from their combined influence, is examined by analyzing the posterior distributions of key CS properties, nuclear characteristics at saturation density, and their correlations. Results: Our systematic Bayesian analysis, which incorporates the PSR J0952--0607 and/or HESS J1731--347 data alongside typical astrophysical constraints, indicates that our CDF models can support higher maximum masses for CSs, reaching up to <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mn>2.4</mml:mn><mml:mtext>--</mml:mtext><mml:mn>2.5</mml:mn><mml:mspace></mml:mspace><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula>. However, achieving sufficient softening of the EoS in the low-density regime to accommodate the HESS J1731--347 data remains challenging. Nonetheless, we are able to impose tighter constraints on the parameter space of CDF models, ensuring consistency with current nuclear experimental and astrophysical data. Conclusions: CDF models with density-dependent meson-nucleon couplings encompass a wide range of nuclear and astrophysical phenomena, providing a robust theoretical framework for interpreting compact objects. However, the predicted lower limit for the radii of low-mass stars is approximately 12 km, which stems from the restricted degrees of freedom in the isovector sector. These limitations can be addressed by extending CDF models to introduce more complex density dependence of the symmetry energy. Additionally, smaller radii may be achieved by allowing for a low-density deconfinement phase transition to quark matter.

</details>

*Wu, Yu-Mei,Bi, Yan-Chen,Huang, Qing-Guo*

Abstract: In the nanohertz band, the spatial correlations in pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) produced by interfering gravitational waves (GWs) from multiple sources likely deviate from the traditional ones without interference under the assumption of an isotropic Gaussian ensemble. This work investigates the impact of such interference within the framework of massive gravity. Through simulations, we show that while the resulting correlation patterns can be described by Legendre expansions with coefficients that depend on the interference configuration, they remain predominantly quadrupolar (<inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mi>l</mml:mi><mml:mo>=</mml:mo><mml:mn>2</mml:mn></mml:math></inline-formula>), with this feature becoming more pronounced as the graviton mass increases---reflecting both the tensorial polarizations and the modified GW dispersion. However, the interference introduces significant variability in the angular correlation, making it difficult to distinguish massive gravity from general relativity based on a single realization of the Universe. We conclude that beyond a fundamental constraint set by the PTA observation time, achieving a substantially tighter bound on the graviton mass is statistically challenging and observationally limited under realistic conditions.

The data analysis of SGWB involves cross-correlating the outputs of a pair of detectors to find the functional form of the correlations, which is referred to as the overlap reduction function (ORF). While conventional approaches employ detector-specific approximations for ORF calculations, in this paper, we present a universal formula to calculate the precise ORF for isotropic SGWB detection with PTA and laser interferometer detectors. Since no approximations are employed, the exact value of the ORF can be obtained for all three types of detectors.

Using a suite of three-dimensional, force-free, general-relativistic solutions for dipole and dipole-plus-quadrupole fields, we find that space-based interferometers may enable marginal detections out to <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:mo>≲</mml:mo><mml:mi>kpc</mml:mi></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> distances for slowly rotating magnetars with fields of <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mo>≳</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mn>15</mml:mn></mml:msup><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mtext> </mml:mtext><mml:mi>G</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula> independently of internal deformations.

Abstract: The possibility of compact stars as hideouts for color-spin-locked (CSL) quark matter (QM) is investigated in both the MIT bag model and the Nambu-Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model. Within the framework of the NJL model, the idea of absolutely stable quark matter and the existence of the conventional pure quark star (QS) are not supported; in addition, there appears to be no stable hybrid configuration above <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mn>2</mml:mn><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> as the hideout for CSL QM. The stable configurations of massive strange quark stars could be reproduced in the MIT bag model with QCD corrections being taken into account; moreover, they could act as the hiding place for the CSL QM. An interesting scenario is proposed that the phase transition to the CSL phase could occur in the cooling process. The CSL quark matter is an electromagnetic (EM) superconductor of type I, and a complete Meissner effect is expected. However, the analysis for this sizable superconductor indicates that most of the magnetic field is frozen inside the quark core with a critical strength, while in some special cases a small fraction could be expelled from a thin layer near the surface in a short time. The analysis on energetics and timescale suggests that this process could act as an inducement mechanism to power typical fast radio bursts, but as a single source of energy, it is unlikely to generate other EM emissions such as gamma-ray bursts and giant flares.

Abstract: Multiple pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) have recently reported evidence for nHz stochastic gravitational wave background (SGWB), stimulating intensive discussions about its physical origin. In principle, the sources may be either supermassive black hole binaries (SMBHBs) or processes in the early Universe. One key difference between the two lies in the statistics of the SGWB frequency power spectrum. In particular, the often assumed Gaussian random SGWB does not accurately describe the distribution of the collective SMBHB emission. This work presents a semianalytical framework for calculating the non-Gaussian statistics of SGWB power expected from SMBHBs. We find that (a) wave interference between individual SMBHBs with indistinguishable observed frequencies and (b) the Poisson fluctuation of the source numbers, together shape the non-Gaussian statistics. Implementing the non-Gaussian statistics developed in this work, we investigate the sensitivity of current and future PTA datasets in distinguishing the origin of the SGWB through non-Gaussian information. Additionally, we find an interesting approximation of the non-Gaussian statistics, which has implications for accurately and practically treating non-Gaussianity in PTA Bayesian analyses.

</details>

Abstract: Resonant axion-plasmon conversion in the magnetospheres of magnetars may substantially impact the landscape of dark-matter axion detection. This Letter explores how resonant axion-plasmon conversion modify the expected radio signals from axion-photon conversions observed on Earth. Critically, the resonant conversion radius lies within the region expected for axion-photon conversion, introducing a nonradiative power loss that diminishes the anticipated photon flux. Our analysis demonstrates that this effect can reduce radio telescope sensitivities, shifting them into regions excluded by previous experiments. These findings compel a reassessment of experimental constraints derived from radio signatures of axion-photon conversions and highlight the necessity of accounting for plasmon effects in astrophysical axion searches. The presented corrections provide critical insights for refining the detection strategies of future telescope-based dark matter axion experiments.

Conclusion: 作者提出，通过构造最优估计量，可以在高斯系综源假设下预言HD相关性的方差，并将其解释为脉冲星天空位置相关的分子与引力波信号主导噪声的有效频率bin数目之比；由此，经过适当组合，每个这样的频率bin提供一个独立的HD相关性估计。这些结论是否可推广到其他源系综或实际数据分析，根据摘要无法判断；与磁星、快速射电暴或单脉冲发射等主题的联系，摘要未说明。

Abstract: Pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) detect gravitational waves (GWs) via the correlations they create in the arrival times of pulses from different pulsars. The mean correlation, a function of the angle between the directions to two pulsars, was predicted in 1983 by Hellings and Downs (HD). Observation of this angular pattern is crucial evidence that GWs are present, so PTAs ``reconstruct the HD curve'' by estimating the correlation using pulsar pairs separated by similar angles. Several studies have examined the amount by which this curve is expected to differ from the HD mean. The variance arises because (a) a finite set of pulsars at specific sky locations is used, (b) the GW sources interfere, and (c) the data are contaminated by noise. Here, for a Gaussian ensemble of sources, we predict that variance by constructing an optimal estimator of the HD correlation, taking into account the pulsar sky locations and the frequency distribution of the GWs and the pulsar noise. The variance is a ratio: the numerator depends upon the pulsar sky locations, and the denominator is the (effective) number of frequency bins for which the GW signal dominates the noise. In effect, after suitable combination, each such frequency bin gives an independent estimate of the HD correlation.

</details>

Conclusion: 作者认为，所提出的共轭类Jeffreys多元先验加Gibbs采样方案可作为最一般、不依赖模型的逐频贝叶斯手段处理PTA红噪声协方差矩阵参数，并与更受限的标准技术在恢复自谱与互谱上保持一致；同时指出可利用单脉冲星与共同不相关红噪声分析的傅里叶系数估计，经特定逆Wishart分布解析抽样近似刻画GWB。其物理推论及对脉冲星、磁星、单脉冲、快速射电暴等的适用性，根据摘要无法判断。

Abstract: The announcement in the summer of 2023 about the discovery of evidence for a gravitational-wave background (GWB) using pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) ignited interest in both the PTA and larger scientific communities about the experiment itself and the scientific implications of its findings. As a result, numerous scientific works have been published analyzing and further developing various aspects of the experiment, from performing tests of gravity to improving the efficiency of the current data analysis techniques. In this regard, we contribute to the recent advancements in the field of PTAs by presenting the most general, agnostic, per-frequency Bayesian search for a low-frequency (red) noise process in these data. Our new method involves the use of a conjugate Jeffreys-like multivariate prior, which allows one to model all unique parameters of the global PTA-level red-noise covariance matrix as a separate model parameter for which a marginalized posterior-probability distribution can be found using Gibbs sampling. Even though perfecting the implementation of the Gibbs sampling and mitigating the numerical stability challenges require further development, we show the power of this new method by analyzing realistic and theoretical PTA simulated data sets. We show how our technique is consistent with the more restricted standard techniques in recovering both the auto and cross spectra of pulsars' low-frequency (red) noise. Furthermore, we highlight ways to approximately characterize a GWB (both its auto and cross spectra) using Fourier coefficient estimates from single-pulsar and so-called common uncorrelated red-noise analyses via analytic draws from a specific inverse Wishart distribution.

</details>

Method: 本文扩展了PTA最优统计量（OS）的能力，提出逐频最优统计量（PFOS），去除先前OS实现中固有的幂律假设，以逐频方式估计GWB谱，并将OS流程近期的一项推广适配进PFOS。摘要未说明具体的观测数据、望远镜、频段或时间跨度；结果基于模拟给出。

Abstract: Pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) have made tremendous progress and are now showing strong evidence for the gravitational-wave background (GWB). Further probing the origin and characteristics of the GWB will require more generalized analysis techniques. Bayesian methods are most often used but can be computationally expensive. On the other hand, frequentist methods, like the PTA optimal statistic (OS), are more computationally efficient and can produce results that are complementary to Bayesian methods, allowing for stronger statistical cases to be built from a confluence of different approaches. In this work we expand the capabilities of the OS through a technique we call the per-frequency optimal statistic (PFOS). The PFOS removes the underlying power-law assumption inherent in previous implementations of the OS, and allows one to estimate the GWB spectrum in a frequency-by-frequency manner. We have also adapted a recent generalization from the OS pipeline into the PFOS, making it capable of accurately characterizing the spectrum in the intermediate and strong GW signal regimes using only a small fraction of the necessary computational resources when compared with fully correlated Bayesian methods, while also empowering many new types of analyses not possible before. We find that even in the strong GW signal regime, where the GWB dominates over noise in all frequencies, the injected value of the signal lies within the 50th percentile of the PFOS uncertainty distribution in 41--45% of simulations, remaining <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mn>3</mml:mn><mml:mi>σ</mml:mi></mml:math></inline-formula> consistent with unbiased estimation.

</details>

Abstract: Primordial black holes (PBHs) in the mass range <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:mrow><mml:msup><mml:mrow><mml:mn>10</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>16</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:msup><mml:mrow><mml:mn>10</mml:mn></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>11</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:msub><mml:mrow><mml:mi>M</mml:mi></mml:mrow><mml:mrow><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:mrow></mml:msub></mml:mrow></mml:math></inline-formula> may constitute all the dark matter. We show that gravitational microlensing of bright x-ray pulsars provide the most robust and immediately implementable opportunity to uncover PBH dark matter in this mass window. As proof of concept, we show that the currently operational NICER telescope can probe this window near <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>14</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> with just two months of exposure on the x-ray pulsar SMC-X1, and that the forthcoming STROBE-X telescope can probe complementary regions in only a few weeks. These times are comparable to the week-long exposures obtained by NICER on various individual sources. We take into account the effects of wave optics and the finite extent of the source, which become important for the mass range of our PBHs. We also provide a spectral diagnostic to distinguish microlensing from transient background events and to broadly mark the PBH mass if true microlensing events are observed. In light of the powerful science case, i.e., the imminent discovery of dark matter searchable over multiple decades of PBH masses with achievable exposures, we strongly urge the commission of a dedicated large broadband telescope for x-ray microlensing. We derive the microlensing reach of such a telescope by assuming sensitivities of detector components of proposed missions and find that with hard x-ray pulsar sources PBH masses down to a few <inline-formula><mml:math><mml:msup><mml:mn>10</mml:mn><mml:mrow><mml:mo>-</mml:mo><mml:mn>17</mml:mn></mml:mrow></mml:msup><mml:msub><mml:mi>M</mml:mi><mml:mo>⊙</mml:mo></mml:msub></mml:math></inline-formula> can be probed.
